YELLOW

A landmark may be more useful than a badly estimated
bearing. “
F, Angular Elevation of Object
Estimated heights are unreliable
G. Distance of object from Observer NOT KNOWN, FAR A way/
By reference to a known landmark wherever possible.

Changes in E, F and G may be of more use than
estimates of course and speed

steeples; spires; TV or radio masts; airfields; generating
plant; factories; pits or other sites with floodlights or other |.

+** FILE NOTE ***

On the evening of 8 October 1998 Earth passed through debris from
a comet resulting in a show of shooting stars. Any sightings taken
on this date between 6pm and midnight could be accounted for by
this.

THERE was a close encounter
for those who spotted strange
flashes of light darting through
the sky.

People living at Felinheli,
Rhiwlas and Waunfawr alerted
RAF Valley on Anglesey to the
weird phenomenon, thinking there
was a UFO invasion. :

It almost became a case for
Mulder and Scully as flashes of
light came from the direction of

Bangor like a scene from.

television's The X-Files.
But after a few of our own
investigations, the Mail discovered

the truth was out there -—on top of -

Bangor University’s Students’
Union!

Shane Little:
‘flower light’ which we were ~
expecting to cause interest.

projection which has -10 beams
which spin round and can be seen
up to eight or nine miles away.

“The lights arrived just in time’

for Freshers week and we tried
them out on Monday night which
is our biggest night where we play
60s, 70s and 80s music.

“We've had planning permission

‘ phenomen6

of Bangor.

the public but we were expecting «

them and we hoped it would cause
interest.

RAF Valley Press liaison officer

if itcan be easily explained.”

b. Date, time and duration of sighting: 12 August 1998 at 2200 hrs, duration of

apporximately 5 minutes.

c, Description of object: Four main lights formed a cross. Other less bright lights were in
and arouad the four main ones. One much brighter light was in the centre; and was either
green or orange. The object was very low, just above the tops of the trees, and appeared to be
considerably larger than a 747. The object hovered for about 5 minutes and was absolutely

silent.

d. Exact position of observer: The observer was driving back to Barry from Cardiff along
the A4050 with her husband, daughter and daughter’s fiancé. They pulled off the road.as soon
as possible onto the Myther Duvan road. The object was on the right side, over towards the
Wenvoe golf course.

e. How observed: (naked eye, binoculars, other optical device, still or video camera).
Observed by naked.

st seen: (A landmark may be more useful than a

£ Direction in whi

badly estimated bearing).

5. Angle of sight:

h Distance: (By reference to a known landmark wherever possible)
200-300 feet.

I. Movements: Hovered for about five minutes before shooting off very fast, up and over
towards Cardiff, Culverhouse Cross.

j. Meteorological conditions during observations:(Moving clouds, haze, mist ¢t¢).

Clear night sky

k, Nearby objects: (Telephone lines, high voltage lines; reservoir, lake or dam; swamp or
marsh; river, high buildings; tall chimneys, steeples; spires, TV or radio miasts, airfields;
generating plant; factories; pits or other sights with floodlights or other night lighting).

Only trees.

7 Aug, 2140 hrs, about 5 minutes

Like a shooting star, but with unsyncronised
flashing lights

outdoors

estimate

on itself and returned in the reverse direction

REPORT OF AIRBORNE PHENOMENON

$i |
1. Flight Lieutenan’ (ggliall  s handed me the details of your
sighting from 18 July 1998 for further analysis. Very shortly
after you called Fylingdales, the information was signalled to an
office in the Ministry of Defence which deals with all such
reports. Their sole task is to check that the incident does not
represent a direct threat to the security of the United Kingdom.
If they decide that a threat does not exist, they have no further
responsibility to determine the cause or logic of the event.
However, as a courtesy, I try to write to people making the report
to assure them that their concern was not ignored.

23 Following your cail some analysis was possible and it looks as
if the object you saw was probably an American satellite. It is
cylindrical and has large solar sails, or panels with solar cells
to charge the batteries with the sun’s energy. It was certainly in
the right place for you to have seen it then. The satellite would
have been in daylight while you were in gathering darkness and the
solar sails would have been pointed at the sun for maximum effect.
You would have seen the sun reflected in these mirror-like plates.

3. Unfortunately, the Whitby Gazette reporter spoke to Fyling-

dales before the result of the analysis was available so you now

have the authentic answer. I hope the sighting did not cause too
muc nyxiety.

Flight Lieutenant
For Officer Commanding
